Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `comb_sop_2_18` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
- input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
- output ports: result[64]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: none; the block is combinational, and `result` is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, stereovision1.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 4 products in one tree of adders.

idinstances
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision1.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: per instance, the adder lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg that feeds corr_out_tmp (module corr, line 1385; module corr_seq, line 1869). The remaining half of the 4-product tree (the p/n addition into corr_out_1*/corr_out_2*/corr_out_4*) stays in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem and is not touched.
BLOCKS: 76
MAPPING: one comb_sop_2_18 per corr instance (64) and per corr_seq instance (12). In corr: ax = {10'b0, in_l_re}, ay = {11'b0, in_r_re}, bx = {10'b0, in_l_im}, by = {11'b0, in_r_im}, result[15:0] -> new register sop_reg. In corr_seq: ax = {10'b0, in_l_re_reg}, ay = {11'b0, in_r_re_reg}, bx = {10'b0, in_l_im_reg}, by = {11'b0, in_r_im_reg}, result[15:0] -> new register sop_reg. All four input ports are driven in both cases; no port is tied to 0. result[63:16] is left unconnected.
PLAN:

What is being converted

All four candidate ids are converted. They are not four separate rewrites: ids …:1374$78 and …:1375$79 are the two products of one corr instance, and …:1867$104 / …:1868$105 are the two products of one corr_seq instance. Each pair becomes exactly one block, so 64 + 12 = 76 blocks, 76 DSP tiles of the 306 available. No candidate region is left unconverted.

Per converted instance the block absorbs: two 8x8 unsigned multipliers and the 16-bit adder that sums them. Across the design that removes 152 soft 8x8 multipliers, 76 soft 16-bit adders, and 76x16 = 1216 flip-flops (lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg, 32 FFs per instance, are replaced by one 16-bit sop_reg).

Why this is behaviour-preserving
  • Operands are 8 bits (sh_reg_w = 8 everywhere; no instance overrides it — see lines 1247-1267, 1590-1610, 1734-1739). in_l_re * in_r_re is an unsigned 8x8 product that fits exactly in the 16-bit lrexrre_reg, so no original product is truncated. The block reads its ports unsigned and zero-extends, which matches.
  • corr_out_tmp <= lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg is a 16-bit add that wraps mod 2^16. The block's result is the exact 64-bit sum of the same two exact products, so result[15:0] equals the original 16-bit sum bit for bit.
  • Visibility: lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg and corr_out_tmp are local and each has exactly one reader (grep over the whole file confirms: lines 1367-1369/1374-1385 and 1844-1846/1867-1869 are the only occurrences). Nothing outside the module reads a product or the pair-sum, so both products and their sum may go inside the block. The p-side and n-side corr_out values are separately visible (they leave wrapper_norm_corr_20 / _10 / _5_seq as ports and are read individually at lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405), so the other two products of the 4-product tree cannot be pulled into the same block — hence one block per corr/corr_seq, not one per p/n pair.
  • Registers/reset: corr and corr_seq have no reset. The register that is moved is replaced one-for-one at the same pipeline stage, so there is nothing reset-dependent to reproduce.
  • No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is exact for all inputs.
Register stages, before and after

corr (64 instances, lines 1352-1387). Before: operands in_l_re/in_r_re/in_l_im/in_r_im -> (products registered) lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg -> (sum registered) corr_out_tmp = 2 stages; corr_out is a third, gated by new_data. After: operands -> block (combinational) -> (registered) sop_reg -> (registered) corr_out_tmp = 2 stages; corr_out unchanged as the third. Latency identical; the register that sat between the products and their sum now sits after the sum.

corr_seq (12 instances, lines 1829-1871). Before: in_*_reg (the new_data-enabled input registers) -> lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg -> corr_out_tmp = 2 stages, i.e. 3 stages from the module inputs. After: in_*_reg -> block -> sop_reg -> corr_out_tmp = 2 stages, 3 from the module inputs. The enabled input registers in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg are kept exactly as they are and feed the block; they are not moved into it.

This register move across the adder is what makes the rewrite T2 rather than T1.

Edits for the editor

Do not add a comb_sop_2_18 module definition to stereovision1.v — only instantiate it. The behavioural model is supplied separately (sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v) and the architecture supplies the block.

Make exactly two textual edits; every instance is covered because all 76 are instances of these two modules.

Edit 1 — module corr, lines 1352-1387.

Replace the declarations at lines 1367-1369:

verilog
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] sop_reg;
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] corr_out_tmp;
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

(lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg are deleted. Leave the four unused in_*_reg wire declarations at lines 1363-1366 alone — they are already dangling in the original.)

Add, before the always block:

verilog
    comb_sop_2_18 corr_sop_inst (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re}),
        .ay({11'b0, in_r_re}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im}),
        .by({11'b0, in_r_im}),

    );

    begin

          end

Edit 2 — module corr_seq, lines 1829-1871.

    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] sop_reg;
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] corr_out_tmp;
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

Keep the four reg[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_*_reg declarations at lines 1840-1843 and their enabled assignments at lines 1850-1865 exactly as they are.

Add, before the always block:

verilog
    comb_sop_2_18 corr_seq_sop_inst (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re_reg}),
        .ay({11'b0, in_r_re_reg}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im_reg}),
        .by({11'b0, in_r_im_reg}),
        .result(sop_res)

Replace lines 1866-1869 (the two multiply assignments and the sum) with:

verilog
             sop_reg <= sop_res[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0];
          corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg ;

Everything else in both modules, and every other module in the file, is unchanged.

Power-up note

sop_reg takes the pipeline slot of lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg, which power up at 0 in the original. It must be a plain soft register with no initial value in the source; the testbench's $init_zero sets the power-up value of every soft register in the DUT, so it powers up at 0 exactly as the registers it replaces do, and cycle 1's corr_out_tmp is 0 in both designs. Do not add an initial block for it.

Expected effect on area x delay

Area: 76 DSP tiles (76 x 253779 = 19.3M MWTA) replace 152 soft 8x8 multipliers, 76 soft 16-bit adders and 1216 FFs. On a soft-multiplier baseline (~70-90 LUTs per 8x8 unsigned multiply) that is roughly 12k LUTs plus their routing removed, well over the DSP area added, at a CLB cost of 27905 MWTA.

Delay: the new critical stage inside corr/corr_seq is soft-FF clk-to-Q (0.060) + block (2.14) = 2.20 ns plus routing, and the following stage (sop_reg -> corr_out_tmp) is a bare wire. This beats the original stage-1 path (FF -> soft 8x8 multiply) if that multiply costs more than ~2.14 ns, which is the expected case for a LUT-mapped 8x8 array; if the baseline multiply is cheaper than the block, this stage becomes the limiter and the framework's measurement will discard the candidate and keep the original.

DESIGN_CHANGE: in both corr and corr_seq the stage between the products and their sum exists only to break the soft multiplier's delay; the block does both products and the sum in one 2.14 ns combinational path. If that pipeline stage were allowed to be removed (dropping one cycle of latency on corr_out, which changes the design's timing and so is out of scope here), the rewrite would also drop 76 x 16 = 1216 further flip-flops and the sop_reg stage entirely, with no delay cost.

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is stereovision1.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 4 products in one tree of adders.

idinstances
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: per instance, the adder lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg that feeds corr_out_tmp (module corr, line 1385; module corr_seq, line 1869). The remaining half of the 4-product tree (the p/n addition into corr_out_1*/corr_out_2*/corr_out_4*) stays in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem and is not touched.
BLOCKS: 76
MAPPING: one comb_sop_2_18 per corr instance (64) and per corr_seq instance (12). In corr: ax = {10'b0, in_l_re}, ay = {11'b0, in_r_re}, bx = {10'b0, in_l_im}, by = {11'b0, in_r_im}, result[15:0] -> new register sop_reg. In corr_seq: ax = {10'b0, in_l_re_reg}, ay = {11'b0, in_r_re_reg}, bx = {10'b0, in_l_im_reg}, by = {11'b0, in_r_im_reg}, result[15:0] -> new register sop_reg. All four input ports are driven in both cases; no port is tied to 0. result[63:16] is left unconnected.
PLAN:

What is being converted

All four candidate ids are converted. They are not four separate rewrites: ids …:1374$78 and …:1375$79 are the two products of one corr instance, and …:1867$104 / …:1868$105 are the two products of one corr_seq instance. Each pair becomes exactly one block, so 64 + 12 = 76 blocks, 76 DSP tiles of the 306 available. No candidate region is left unconverted.

Per converted instance the block absorbs: two 8x8 unsigned multipliers and the 16-bit adder that sums them. Across the design that removes 152 soft 8x8 multipliers, 76 soft 16-bit adders, and 76x16 = 1216 flip-flops (lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg, 32 FFs per instance, are replaced by one 16-bit sop_reg).

Why this is behaviour-preserving
  • Operands are 8 bits (sh_reg_w = 8 everywhere; no instance overrides it — see lines 1247-1267, 1590-1610, 1734-1739). in_l_re * in_r_re is an unsigned 8x8 product that fits exactly in the 16-bit lrexrre_reg, so no original product is truncated. The block reads its ports unsigned and zero-extends, which matches.
  • corr_out_tmp <= lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg is a 16-bit add that wraps mod 2^16. The block's result is the exact 64-bit sum of the same two exact products, so result[15:0] equals the original 16-bit sum bit for bit.
  • Visibility: lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg and corr_out_tmp are local and each has exactly one reader (grep over the whole file confirms: lines 1367-1369/1374-1385 and 1844-1846/1867-1869 are the only occurrences). Nothing outside the module reads a product or the pair-sum, so both products and their sum may go inside the block. The p-side and n-side corr_out values are separately visible (they leave wrapper_norm_corr_20 / _10 / _5_seq as ports and are read individually at lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405), so the other two products of the 4-product tree cannot be pulled into the same block — hence one block per corr/corr_seq, not one per p/n pair.
  • Registers/reset: corr and corr_seq have no reset. The register that is moved is replaced one-for-one at the same pipeline stage, so there is nothing reset-dependent to reproduce.
  • No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is exact for all inputs.
Register stages, before and after

corr (64 instances, lines 1352-1387). Before: operands in_l_re/in_r_re/in_l_im/in_r_im -> (products registered) lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg -> (sum registered) corr_out_tmp = 2 stages; corr_out is a third, gated by new_data. After: operands -> block (combinational) -> (registered) sop_reg -> (registered) corr_out_tmp = 2 stages; corr_out unchanged as the third. Latency identical; the register that sat between the products and their sum now sits after the sum.

corr_seq (12 instances, lines 1829-1871). Before: in_*_reg (the new_data-enabled input registers) -> lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg -> corr_out_tmp = 2 stages, i.e. 3 stages from the module inputs. After: in_*_reg -> block -> sop_reg -> corr_out_tmp = 2 stages, 3 from the module inputs. The enabled input registers in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg are kept exactly as they are and feed the block; they are not moved into it.

This register move across the adder is what makes the rewrite T2 rather than T1.

Edits for the editor

Do not add a comb_sop_2_18 module definition to stereovision1.v — only instantiate it. The behavioural model is supplied separately (sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v) and the architecture supplies the block.

Make exactly two textual edits; every instance is covered because all 76 are instances of these two modules.

Edit 1 — module corr, lines 1352-1387.

Replace the declarations at lines 1367-1369:

verilog
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] sop_reg;
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] corr_out_tmp;
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

(lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg are deleted. Leave the four unused in_*_reg wire declarations at lines 1363-1366 alone — they are already dangling in the original.)

    comb_sop_2_18 corr_sop_inst (
        .ax({10'b0, in_l_re}),
        .ay({11'b0, in_r_re}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im}),
        .by({11'b0, in_r_im}),

    );

    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] corr_out_tmp;
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

Keep the four reg[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_*_reg declarations at lines 1840-1843 and their enabled assignments at lines 1850-1865 exactly as they are.

        .ay({11'b0, in_r_re_reg}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im_reg}),
        .by({11'b0, in_r_im_reg}),
        .result(sop_res)

Replace lines 1866-1869 (the two multiply assignments and the sum) with:

          corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg ;

Everything else in both modules, and every other module in the file, is unchanged.

Power-up note

sop_reg takes the pipeline slot of lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg, which power up at 0 in the original. It must be a plain soft register with no initial value in the source; the testbench's $init_zero sets the power-up value of every soft register in the DUT, so it powers up at 0 exactly as the registers it replaces do, and cycle 1's corr_out_tmp is 0 in both designs. Do not add an initial block for it.

Expected effect on area x delay

Area: 76 DSP tiles (76 x 253779 = 19.3M MWTA) replace 152 soft 8x8 multipliers, 76 soft 16-bit adders and 1216 FFs. On a soft-multiplier baseline (~70-90 LUTs per 8x8 unsigned multiply) that is roughly 12k LUTs plus their routing removed, well over the DSP area added, at a CLB cost of 27905 MWTA.

Delay: the new critical stage inside corr/corr_seq is soft-FF clk-to-Q (0.060) + block (2.14) = 2.20 ns plus routing, and the following stage (sop_reg -> corr_out_tmp) is a bare wire. This beats the original stage-1 path (FF -> soft 8x8 multiply) if that multiply costs more than ~2.14 ns, which is the expected case for a LUT-mapped 8x8 array; if the baseline multiply is cheaper than the block, this stage becomes the limiter and the framework's measurement will discard the candidate and keep the original.

DESIGN_CHANGE: in both corr and corr_seq the stage between the products and their sum exists only to break the soft multiplier's delay; the block does both products and the sum in one 2.14 ns combinational path. If that pipeline stage were allowed to be removed (dropping one cycle of latency on corr_out, which changes the design's timing and so is out of scope here), the rewrite would also drop 76 x 16 = 1216 further flip-flops and the sop_reg stage entirely, with no delay cost.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14	
15	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax * ay;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
17	    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact
18	
19	    assign result = sum;
20	
21	endmodule
22

prompt
Task

Edit stereovision1.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem). The plan names their shared sum as 'per instance, the adder lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg that feeds corr_out_tmp (module corr, line 1385; module corr_seq, line 1869). The remaining half of the 4-product tree (the p/n addition into corr_out_1*/corr_out_2*/corr_out_4*) stays in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem and is not touched.'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh stereovision1.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: per instance, the adder lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg that feeds corr_out_tmp (module corr, line 1385; module corr_seq, line 1869). The remaining half of the 4-product tree (the p/n addition into corr_out_1*/corr_out_2*/corr_out_4*) stays in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem and is not touched.
BLOCKS: 76
MAPPING: one comb_sop_2_18 per corr instance (64) and per corr_seq instance (12). In corr: ax = {10'b0, in_l_re}, ay = {11'b0, in_r_re}, bx = {10'b0, in_l_im}, by = {11'b0, in_r_im}, result[15:0] -> new register sop_reg. In corr_seq: ax = {10'b0, in_l_re_reg}, ay = {11'b0, in_r_re_reg}, bx = {10'b0, in_l_im_reg}, by = {11'b0, in_r_im_reg}, result[15:0] -> new register sop_reg. All four input ports are driven in both cases; no port is tied to 0. result[63:16] is left unconnected.
PLAN:

What is being converted

All four candidate ids are converted. They are not four separate rewrites: ids …:1374$78 and …:1375$79 are the two products of one corr instance, and …:1867$104 / …:1868$105 are the two products of one corr_seq instance. Each pair becomes exactly one block, so 64 + 12 = 76 blocks, 76 DSP tiles of the 306 available. No candidate region is left unconverted.

Per converted instance the block absorbs: two 8x8 unsigned multipliers and the 16-bit adder that sums them. Across the design that removes 152 soft 8x8 multipliers, 76 soft 16-bit adders, and 76x16 = 1216 flip-flops (lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg, 32 FFs per instance, are replaced by one 16-bit sop_reg).

Why this is behaviour-preserving
  • Operands are 8 bits (sh_reg_w = 8 everywhere; no instance overrides it — see lines 1247-1267, 1590-1610, 1734-1739). in_l_re * in_r_re is an unsigned 8x8 product that fits exactly in the 16-bit lrexrre_reg, so no original product is truncated. The block reads its ports unsigned and zero-extends, which matches.
  • corr_out_tmp <= lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg is a 16-bit add that wraps mod 2^16. The block's result is the exact 64-bit sum of the same two exact products, so result[15:0] equals the original 16-bit sum bit for bit.
  • Visibility: lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg and corr_out_tmp are local and each has exactly one reader (grep over the whole file confirms: lines 1367-1369/1374-1385 and 1844-1846/1867-1869 are the only occurrences). Nothing outside the module reads a product or the pair-sum, so both products and their sum may go inside the block. The p-side and n-side corr_out values are separately visible (they leave wrapper_norm_corr_20 / _10 / _5_seq as ports and are read individually at lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405), so the other two products of the 4-product tree cannot be pulled into the same block — hence one block per corr/corr_seq, not one per p/n pair.
  • Registers/reset: corr and corr_seq have no reset. The register that is moved is replaced one-for-one at the same pipeline stage, so there is nothing reset-dependent to reproduce.
  • No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is exact for all inputs.
Register stages, before and after

corr (64 instances, lines 1352-1387). Before: operands in_l_re/in_r_re/in_l_im/in_r_im -> (products registered) lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg -> (sum registered) corr_out_tmp = 2 stages; corr_out is a third, gated by new_data. After: operands -> block (combinational) -> (registered) sop_reg -> (registered) corr_out_tmp = 2 stages; corr_out unchanged as the third. Latency identical; the register that sat between the products and their sum now sits after the sum.

corr_seq (12 instances, lines 1829-1871). Before: in_*_reg (the new_data-enabled input registers) -> lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg -> corr_out_tmp = 2 stages, i.e. 3 stages from the module inputs. After: in_*_reg -> block -> sop_reg -> corr_out_tmp = 2 stages, 3 from the module inputs. The enabled input registers in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg are kept exactly as they are and feed the block; they are not moved into it.

This register move across the adder is what makes the rewrite T2 rather than T1.

Edits for the editor

Do not add a comb_sop_2_18 module definition to stereovision1.v — only instantiate it. The behavioural model is supplied separately (sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v) and the architecture supplies the block.

Make exactly two textual edits; every instance is covered because all 76 are instances of these two modules.

Edit 1 — module corr, lines 1352-1387.

        .ay({11'b0, in_r_re_reg}),
        .bx({10'b0, in_l_im_reg}),

Replace lines 1866-1869 (the two multiply assignments and the sum) with:

          corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg ;

Everything else in both modules, and every other module in the file, is unchanged.

Power-up note

sop_reg takes the pipeline slot of lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg, which power up at 0 in the original. It must be a plain soft register with no initial value in the source; the testbench's $init_zero sets the power-up value of every soft register in the DUT, so it powers up at 0 exactly as the registers it replaces do, and cycle 1's corr_out_tmp is 0 in both designs. Do not add an initial block for it.

Expected effect on area x delay

Area: 76 DSP tiles (76 x 253779 = 19.3M MWTA) replace 152 soft 8x8 multipliers, 76 soft 16-bit adders and 1216 FFs. On a soft-multiplier baseline (~70-90 LUTs per 8x8 unsigned multiply) that is roughly 12k LUTs plus their routing removed, well over the DSP area added, at a CLB cost of 27905 MWTA.

Delay: the new critical stage inside corr/corr_seq is soft-FF clk-to-Q (0.060) + block (2.14) = 2.20 ns plus routing, and the following stage (sop_reg -> corr_out_tmp) is a bare wire. This beats the original stage-1 path (FF -> soft 8x8 multiply) if that multiply costs more than ~2.14 ns, which is the expected case for a LUT-mapped 8x8 array; if the baseline multiply is cheaper than the block, this stage becomes the limiter and the framework's measurement will discard the candidate and keep the original.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

What changed

Region pair corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78 + corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79 (module corr, line 1352 — 64 instances)

  • The two multiply assignments and corr_out_tmp <= lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg; became sop_reg <= sop_res[2*sh_reg_w-1:0]; and corr_out_tmp <= sop_reg;. The in_*_reg input registers and the new_data branch are unchanged.

Nothing else in the file was touched; the p/n addition in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem stays soft, as the plan specifies. No comb_sop_2_18 module definition was added. No ASSUMES: line is needed — sh_reg_w is 8 at every instantiation (no #(...) override, no defparam), so each 8×8 product is exact in 16 bits and result[15:0] equals the original 16-bit wrapping sum.

Checks

bash check.sh stereovision1.v → OK: synthesizes cleanly, census shows comb_sop_2_18 76 (64 corr + 12 corr_seq) and no $mul cells remain.

I also ran a module-level equivalence sim (iverilog, /tmp/vcheck) of the edited corr and corr_seq against copies of the originals, all registers zero at power-up, 20000 random-stimulus cycles including random new_data: mismatches=0. The full-design testbench in sim_reference/ needs the framework's $init_zero VPI task, so that run is left to the framework.

1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14

16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact

Bash completed cat /work/check.sh
input
{
 "command": "cat /work/check.sh"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

block, input to output2.14

  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file stereovision1.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: per instance, the adder lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg that feeds corr_out_tmp (module corr, line 1385; module corr_seq, line 1869). The remaining half of the 4-product tree (the p/n addition into corr_out_1*/corr_out_2*/corr_out_4*) stays in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem and is not touched.
BLOCKS: 76
MAPPING: one comb_sop_2_18 per corr instance (64) and per corr_seq instance (12). In corr: ax = {10'b0, in_l_re}, ay = {11'b0, in_r_re}, bx = {10'b0, in_l_im}, by = {11'b0, in_r_im}, result[15:0] -> new register sop_reg. In corr_seq: ax = {10'b0, in_l_re_reg}, ay = {11'b0, in_r_re_reg}, bx = {10'b0, in_l_im_reg}, by = {11'b0, in_r_im_reg}, result[15:0] -> new register sop_reg. All four input ports are driven in both cases; no port is tied to 0. result[63:16] is left unconnected.
PLAN:

What is being converted

All four candidate ids are converted. They are not four separate rewrites: ids …:1374$78 and …:1375$79 are the two products of one corr instance, and …:1867$104 / …:1868$105 are the two products of one corr_seq instance. Each pair becomes exactly one block, so 64 + 12 = 76 blocks, 76 DSP tiles of the 306 available. No candidate region is left unconverted.

Per converted instance the block absorbs: two 8x8 unsigned multipliers and the 16-bit adder that sums them. Across the design that removes 152 soft 8x8 multipliers, 76 soft 16-bit adders, and 76x16 = 1216 flip-flops (lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg, 32 FFs per instance, are replaced by one 16-bit sop_reg).

Why this is behaviour-preserving
  • Operands are 8 bits (sh_reg_w = 8 everywhere; no instance overrides it — see lines 1247-1267, 1590-1610, 1734-1739). in_l_re * in_r_re is an unsigned 8x8 product that fits exactly in the 16-bit lrexrre_reg, so no original product is truncated. The block reads its ports unsigned and zero-extends, which matches.
  • corr_out_tmp <= lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg is a 16-bit add that wraps mod 2^16. The block's result is the exact 64-bit sum of the same two exact products, so result[15:0] equals the original 16-bit sum bit for bit.
  • Visibility: lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg and corr_out_tmp are local and each has exactly one reader (grep over the whole file confirms: lines 1367-1369/1374-1385 and 1844-1846/1867-1869 are the only occurrences). Nothing outside the module reads a product or the pair-sum, so both products and their sum may go inside the block. The p-side and n-side corr_out values are separately visible (they leave wrapper_norm_corr_20 / _10 / _5_seq as ports and are read individually at lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405), so the other two products of the 4-product tree cannot be pulled into the same block — hence one block per corr/corr_seq, not one per p/n pair.
  • Registers/reset: corr and corr_seq have no reset. The register that is moved is replaced one-for-one at the same pipeline stage, so there is nothing reset-dependent to reproduce.
  • No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is exact for all inputs.
Register stages, before and after

corr (64 instances, lines 1352-1387). Before: operands in_l_re/in_r_re/in_l_im/in_r_im -> (products registered) lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg -> (sum registered) corr_out_tmp = 2 stages; corr_out is a third, gated by new_data. After: operands -> block (combinational) -> (registered) sop_reg -> (registered) corr_out_tmp = 2 stages; corr_out unchanged as the third. Latency identical; the register that sat between the products and their sum now sits after the sum.

corr_seq (12 instances, lines 1829-1871). Before: in_*_reg (the new_data-enabled input registers) -> lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg -> corr_out_tmp = 2 stages, i.e. 3 stages from the module inputs. After: in_*_reg -> block -> sop_reg -> corr_out_tmp = 2 stages, 3 from the module inputs. The enabled input registers in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg are kept exactly as they are and feed the block; they are not moved into it.

This register move across the adder is what makes the rewrite T2 rather than T1.

Edits for the editor

Do not add a comb_sop_2_18 module definition to stereovision1.v — only instantiate it. The behavioural model is supplied separately (sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v) and the architecture supplies the block.

Make exactly two textual edits; every instance is covered because all 76 are instances of these two modules.

Edit 1 — module corr, lines 1352-1387.

Replace the declarations at lines 1367-1369:

verilog
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] sop_reg;
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] corr_out_tmp;
    wire[63:0] sop_res;

Power-up note

sop_reg takes the pipeline slot of lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg, which power up at 0 in the original. It must be a plain soft register with no initial value in the source; the testbench's $init_zero sets the power-up value of every soft register in the DUT, so it powers up at 0 exactly as the registers it replaces do, and cycle 1's corr_out_tmp is 0 in both designs. Do not add an initial block for it.

Expected effect on area x delay

Area: 76 DSP tiles (76 x 253779 = 19.3M MWTA) replace 152 soft 8x8 multipliers, 76 soft 16-bit adders and 1216 FFs. On a soft-multiplier baseline (~70-90 LUTs per 8x8 unsigned multiply) that is roughly 12k LUTs plus their routing removed, well over the DSP area added, at a CLB cost of 27905 MWTA.

Delay: the new critical stage inside corr/corr_seq is soft-FF clk-to-Q (0.060) + block (2.14) = 2.20 ns plus routing, and the following stage (sop_reg -> corr_out_tmp) is a bare wire. This beats the original stage-1 path (FF -> soft 8x8 multiply) if that multiply costs more than ~2.14 ns, which is the expected case for a LUT-mapped 8x8 array; if the baseline multiply is cheaper than the block, this stage becomes the limiter and the framework's measurement will discard the candidate and keep the original.

DESIGN_CHANGE: in both corr and corr_seq the stage between the products and their sum exists only to break the soft multiplier's delay; the block does both products and the sum in one 2.14 ns combinational path. If that pipeline stage were allowed to be removed (dropping one cycle of latency on corr_out, which changes the design's timing and so is out of scope here), the rewrite would also drop 76 x 16 = 1216 further flip-flops and the sop_reg stage entirely, with no delay cost.

Two stages before, two after. Neither module has a reset, so there is nothing reset-dependent to reproduce; the midrun reset pattern cannot diverge them.

Added register — sop_reg occupies the slot of the removed lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg and is declared with no initial value. It self-flushes in one cycle, and the only cycle its power-up value could reach corr_out is the second posedge, which falls inside the testbench reset phase (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v:238-240) with new_data low; $init_zero (tb line 57) sets it to 0 in the DUT regardless. 1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14	
15	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax * ay;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
17	    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact
18	
19	    assign result = sum;
20	
21	endmodule
22

prompt

2 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, stereovision1.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 4 products in one tree of adders.

idinstances
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)12
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

The plan whose measured result is shown

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: per instance, the adder lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg that feeds corr_out_tmp (module corr, line 1385; module corr_seq, line 1869). The remaining half of the 4-product tree (the p/n addition into corr_out_1*/corr_out_2*/corr_out_4*) stays in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem and is not touched.
BLOCKS: 76
MAPPING: one comb_sop_2_18 per corr instance (64) and per corr_seq instance (12). In corr: ax = {10'b0, in_l_re}, ay = {11'b0, in_r_re}, bx = {10'b0, in_l_im}, by = {11'b0, in_r_im}, result[15:0] -> new register sop_reg. In corr_seq: ax = {10'b0, in_l_re_reg}, ay = {11'b0, in_r_re_reg}, bx = {10'b0, in_l_im_reg}, by = {11'b0, in_r_im_reg}, result[15:0] -> new register sop_reg. All four input ports are driven in both cases; no port is tied to 0. result[63:16] is left unconnected.
PLAN:

What is being converted

All four candidate ids are converted. They are not four separate rewrites: ids …:1374$78 and …:1375$79 are the two products of one corr instance, and …:1867$104 / …:1868$105 are the two products of one corr_seq instance. Each pair becomes exactly one block, so 64 + 12 = 76 blocks, 76 DSP tiles of the 306 available. No candidate region is left unconverted.

Per converted instance the block absorbs: two 8x8 unsigned multipliers and the 16-bit adder that sums them. Across the design that removes 152 soft 8x8 multipliers, 76 soft 16-bit adders, and 76x16 = 1216 flip-flops (lrexrre_reg and limxrim_reg, 32 FFs per instance, are replaced by one 16-bit sop_reg).

Why this is behaviour-preserving
  • Operands are 8 bits (sh_reg_w = 8 everywhere; no instance overrides it — see lines 1247-1267, 1590-1610, 1734-1739). in_l_re * in_r_re is an unsigned 8x8 product that fits exactly in the 16-bit lrexrre_reg, so no original product is truncated. The block reads its ports unsigned and zero-extends, which matches.
  • corr_out_tmp <= lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg is a 16-bit add that wraps mod 2^16. The block's result is the exact 64-bit sum of the same two exact products, so result[15:0] equals the original 16-bit sum bit for bit.
  • Visibility: lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg and corr_out_tmp are local and each has exactly one reader (grep over the whole file confirms: lines 1367-1369/1374-1385 and 1844-1846/1867-1869 are the only occurrences). Nothing outside the module reads a product or the pair-sum, so both products and their sum may go inside the block. The p-side and n-side corr_out values are separately visible (they leave wrapper_norm_corr_20 / _10 / _5_seq as ports and are read individually at lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405), so the other two products of the 4-product tree cannot be pulled into the same block — hence one block per corr/corr_seq, not one per p/n pair.
  • Registers/reset: corr and corr_seq have no reset. The register that is moved is replaced one-for-one at the same pipeline stage, so there is nothing reset-dependent to reproduce.
  • No ASSUMES: line is needed; the rewrite is exact for all inputs.
Register stages, before and after

corr (64 instances, lines 1352-1387). Before: operands in_l_re/in_r_re/in_l_im/in_r_im -> (products registered) lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg -> (sum registered) corr_out_tmp = 2 stages; corr_out is a third, gated by new_data. After: operands -> block (combinational) -> (registered) sop_reg -> (registered) corr_out_tmp = 2 stages; corr_out unchanged as the third. Latency identical; the register that sat between the products and their sum now sits after the sum.

corr_seq (12 instances, lines 1829-1871). Before: in_*_reg (the new_data-enabled input registers) -> lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg -> corr_out_tmp = 2 stages, i.e. 3 stages from the module inputs. After: in_*_reg -> block -> sop_reg -> corr_out_tmp = 2 stages, 3 from the module inputs. The enabled input registers in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg are kept exactly as they are and feed the block; they are not moved into it.

This register move across the adder is what makes the rewrite T2 rather than T1.

Edits for the editor

Do not add a comb_sop_2_18 module definition to stereovision1.v — only instantiate it. The behavioural model is supplied separately (sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v) and the architecture supplies the block.

Make exactly two textual edits; every instance is covered because all 76 are instances of these two modules.

Everything else in both modules, and every other module in the file, is unchanged.

Power-up note

sop_reg takes the pipeline slot of lrexrre_reg/limxrim_reg, which power up at 0 in the original. It must be a plain soft register with no initial value in the source; the testbench's $init_zero sets the power-up value of every soft register in the DUT, so it powers up at 0 exactly as the registers it replaces do, and cycle 1's corr_out_tmp is 0 in both designs. Do not add an initial block for it.

Expected effect on area x delay

Area: 76 DSP tiles (76 x 253779 = 19.3M MWTA) replace 152 soft 8x8 multipliers, 76 soft 16-bit adders and 1216 FFs. On a soft-multiplier baseline (~70-90 LUTs per 8x8 unsigned multiply) that is roughly 12k LUTs plus their routing removed, well over the DSP area added, at a CLB cost of 27905 MWTA.

Delay: the new critical stage inside corr/corr_seq is soft-FF clk-to-Q (0.060) + block (2.14) = 2.20 ns plus routing, and the following stage (sop_reg -> corr_out_tmp) is a bare wire. This beats the original stage-1 path (FF -> soft 8x8 multiply) if that multiply costs more than ~2.14 ns, which is the expected case for a LUT-mapped 8x8 array; if the baseline multiply is cheaper than the block, this stage becomes the limiter and the framework's measurement will discard the candidate and keep the original.

DESIGN_CHANGE: in both corr and corr_seq the stage between the products and their sum exists only to break the soft multiplier's delay; the block does both products and the sum in one 2.14 ns combinational path. If that pipeline stage were allowed to be removed (dropping one cycle of latency on corr_out, which changes the design's timing and so is out of scope here), the rewrite would also drop 76 x 16 = 1216 further flip-flops and the sop_reg stage entirely, with no delay cost.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: stereovision1.v

response

I'll read the source before deciding.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: none — no block is instantiated; stereovision1.v stays as given.
PLAN:

Decision

No rewrite onto comb_sop_2_18 is proposed. The first pass was the maximal and the only sensible mapping of these candidates, and the measurement shows the block is strictly more expensive in area than what the architecture already does with this design's multipliers, with no delay gain to pay for it. No smaller or differently grouped variant can reverse that, because the cost is per block and the first pass was already the densest possible packing of products into blocks.

Every candidate, and why each is left

All four candidate ids are left, for one shared reason:

  • corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (64 instances)
  • corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (64 instances)
  • corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (12 instances)
  • corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (12 instances)

Reason: the block is less area-efficient per product than the baseline's own DSP mapping, and it does not shorten the period.

The whole design contains exactly 152 multiply expressions — lines 1374, 1375 (×64 corr instances) and 1867, 1868 (×12 corr_seq instances); a grep for * over the file finds no other multiplication, every remaining hit being a 2 * sh_reg_w width expression. All 152 are unsigned 8×8 (sh_reg_w = 4'b1000 at lines 1354 and 1831, not overridden at any instantiation). The baseline occupies 42 DSP tiles at seed 1, so the baseline already maps those 152 products into hard DSPs at roughly 3.6 products per tile.

comb_sop_2_18 holds 2 products and takes a DSP tile alone. That is about half the baseline's products per tile, so each block converted adds roughly one whole tile (253779 MWTA) while freeing only about two products' share of a baseline tile (~141000) plus one soft 16-bit adder and 16 flip-flops. The measurement confirms the arithmetic: +34 tiles for 76 blocks, area +31.51% (+8.07e6 MWTA, i.e. 34 × 253779 = 8.63e6 less the small soft savings), which is ~106k MWTA added per block converted. The cost is linear in the number of blocks, so converting a subset — one corr, half the instances, corr_seq only — raises area by ~106k per block just the same, with no mechanism to recover it.

There is no delay to recover either. The measured period moved from 5.47292 ns to 5.40569 ns, −1.23%, well inside the ~7% that placement seed alone moves this design's area × delay; it is not evidence of improvement. The post-rewrite critical path reported at seed 1 is FF clk-to-Q 0.060 + 2.045 ns routing into by[6] + 2.140 ns through the block + 1.141 ns routing to $dff~998^Q~15.D — the block is only 2.14 of 5.39 ns, the rest is wire, and the stage it replaced was already a hard DSP multiply in the baseline, not the LUT-mapped array the first pass assumed. So there is no soft-multiplier delay for the block to remove, and the period of the unconverted design is set by paths the block cannot touch.

Area up ~31% at full conversion, ~106k per block at any partial conversion, delay flat: every subset of these candidates raises area × delay. Hence NONE.

Grouping alternatives considered and rejected
  • One block per corr/corr_seq instance (76 blocks) — what the first pass did: the densest grouping available, since the two products of one instance are the only two whose sum (corr_out_tmp, lines 1385 and 1869) is invisible outside the module. Measured at +29.89%.
  • Four products per block — impossible: the block has only two product-term ports, and in any case the p-side and n-side corr_out values of the 4-product tree leave wrapper_norm_corr_20/_10/_5_seq as separate ports and are read individually (lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405), so they cannot be merged into one sum.
  • One product per block, other ports tied to 0 — 152 blocks, 152 tiles, strictly worse than the 76 already measured.
  • Partial conversion (fewer instances) — same +~106k per block with the period unchanged; a smaller loss is still a loss, and the run keeps the original unless a rewrite is below it.
Register stages

No region is converted, so no register stage changes. For the record, the stage counts the first pass produced were already latency-identical to the original (2 stages from operands to corr_out_tmp in both corr and corr_seq), so the +29.89% is purely the DSP-tile cost, not a pipeline effect — nothing is left to reclaim there.

Result the run keeps

The original design. The only rewrite measured in this run, 1.82094e+08, is above the original's 1.40192e+08, so the original stands.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the one change that would make this block competitive here is at the sum, not at the products. Each corr/corr_seq pair's result leaves its wrapper as a separately visible port (corr_out_1*/corr_out_2*/corr_out_4*, read individually at lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405), which is what forces one block per two products. If the p-side and n-side correlations were reduced inside wrapper_norm_corr_* and only the combined value exported, four products would share one reduction and the mapping could put a whole 4-product tree behind two blocks that are then summed once — still 2 products per tile, so it would not by itself beat the baseline's ~3.6 products per tile, but it is the only structural change in the design that would raise the block's usefulness. A block mode that shared a DSP tile with a second block, or one with four product-term ports, would be the architecture-side fix.

The VPR baseline maps all of them to hard DSPs and packs them ~3.6 per tile: 42 DSP tiles at seed 1, baseline area 2.56e7 MWTA, period 5.47 ns.\n\nA `comb_sop_2_18` block takes a DSP tile alone and absorbs only 2 of those products, i.e. ~2x the tile cost per product. Measured (2026-11-03, first pass): converting all 76 corr/corr_seq instances gave 76 tiles (+34), area +31.5%, period -1.2% (inside the ~7% seed noise), area x delay +29.9%. Per block the net cost is ~106k MWTA, so partial conversion is also a loss.\n\n**Why:** the hard block's win on this design would have to come from delay, but the baseline multiply stage is already a DSP, so there is no soft-multiplier delay to remove.\n\n**How to apply:** before planning a sop-block rewrite, check whether the design's products are already DSP-mapped in the baseline and at what packing density; a block that holds fewer products per tile than the native packing cannot win on area.\n"

Objective loop

1 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: planner declined
revisions allowed

original
loop stopped by
planner declined (no representative mapping)
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_small · used area 2.56155e+07 · critical path 5.47292 ns · wirelength 153334 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 1.40192e+08 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 6.95%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 6.95%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

"A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants.
