The plan, in full 5734 chars · not approved

I'll read the source before deciding.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: none — no block is instantiated; stereovision1.v stays as given.
PLAN:

Decision

No rewrite onto comb_sop_2_18 is proposed. The first pass was the maximal and the only sensible mapping of these candidates, and the measurement shows the block is strictly more expensive in area than what the architecture already does with this design's multipliers, with no delay gain to pay for it. No smaller or differently grouped variant can reverse that, because the cost is per block and the first pass was already the densest possible packing of products into blocks.

Every candidate, and why each is left

All four candidate ids are left, for one shared reason:

  • corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (64 instances)
  • corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (64 instances)
  • corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (12 instances)
  • corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem) (12 instances)

Reason: the block is less area-efficient per product than the baseline's own DSP mapping, and it does not shorten the period.

The whole design contains exactly 152 multiply expressions — lines 1374, 1375 (×64 corr instances) and 1867, 1868 (×12 corr_seq instances); a grep for * over the file finds no other multiplication, every remaining hit being a 2 * sh_reg_w width expression. All 152 are unsigned 8×8 (sh_reg_w = 4'b1000 at lines 1354 and 1831, not overridden at any instantiation). The baseline occupies 42 DSP tiles at seed 1, so the baseline already maps those 152 products into hard DSPs at roughly 3.6 products per tile.

comb_sop_2_18 holds 2 products and takes a DSP tile alone. That is about half the baseline's products per tile, so each block converted adds roughly one whole tile (253779 MWTA) while freeing only about two products' share of a baseline tile (~141000) plus one soft 16-bit adder and 16 flip-flops. The measurement confirms the arithmetic: +34 tiles for 76 blocks, area +31.51% (+8.07e6 MWTA, i.e. 34 × 253779 = 8.63e6 less the small soft savings), which is ~106k MWTA added per block converted. The cost is linear in the number of blocks, so converting a subset — one corr, half the instances, corr_seq only — raises area by ~106k per block just the same, with no mechanism to recover it.

There is no delay to recover either. The measured period moved from 5.47292 ns to 5.40569 ns, −1.23%, well inside the ~7% that placement seed alone moves this design's area × delay; it is not evidence of improvement. The post-rewrite critical path reported at seed 1 is FF clk-to-Q 0.060 + 2.045 ns routing into by[6] + 2.140 ns through the block + 1.141 ns routing to $dff~998^Q~15.D — the block is only 2.14 of 5.39 ns, the rest is wire, and the stage it replaced was already a hard DSP multiply in the baseline, not the LUT-mapped array the first pass assumed. So there is no soft-multiplier delay for the block to remove, and the period of the unconverted design is set by paths the block cannot touch.

Area up ~31% at full conversion, ~106k per block at any partial conversion, delay flat: every subset of these candidates raises area × delay. Hence NONE.

Grouping alternatives considered and rejected
  • One block per corr/corr_seq instance (76 blocks) — what the first pass did: the densest grouping available, since the two products of one instance are the only two whose sum (corr_out_tmp, lines 1385 and 1869) is invisible outside the module. Measured at +29.89%.
  • Four products per block — impossible: the block has only two product-term ports, and in any case the p-side and n-side corr_out values of the 4-product tree leave wrapper_norm_corr_20/_10/_5_seq as separate ports and are read individually (lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405), so they cannot be merged into one sum.
  • One product per block, other ports tied to 0 — 152 blocks, 152 tiles, strictly worse than the 76 already measured.
  • Partial conversion (fewer instances) — same +~106k per block with the period unchanged; a smaller loss is still a loss, and the run keeps the original unless a rewrite is below it.
Register stages

No region is converted, so no register stage changes. For the record, the stage counts the first pass produced were already latency-identical to the original (2 stages from operands to corr_out_tmp in both corr and corr_seq), so the +29.89% is purely the DSP-tile cost, not a pipeline effect — nothing is left to reclaim there.

Result the run keeps

The original design. The only rewrite measured in this run, 1.82094e+08, is above the original's 1.40192e+08, so the original stands.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the one change that would make this block competitive here is at the sum, not at the products. Each corr/corr_seq pair's result leaves its wrapper as a separately visible port (corr_out_1*/corr_out_2*/corr_out_4*, read individually at lines 316-336, 368-378, 400-405), which is what forces one block per two products. If the p-side and n-side correlations were reduced inside wrapper_norm_corr_* and only the combined value exported, four products would share one reduction and the mapping could put a whole 4-product tree behind two blocks that are then summed once — still 2 products per tile, so it would not by itself beat the baseline's ~3.6 products per tile, but it is the only structural change in the design that would raise the block's usefulness. A block mode that shared a DSP tile with a second block, or one with four product-term ports, would be the architecture-side fix.

No schematic pair for this rewrite.

Pass 1: area spread 0.0, period spread 0.0000 ns, wirelength spread 0 across the seeds.
